ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u1	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. The meeting was resumed. We continue the debate on the opinion of the Draft Law on General State Budgets for the year 2023. First of all, the debate in section 28, Ministry of Science and Innovation. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister of Science and Innovation. [[Rumors]] Ladies and gentlemen, to start the debate, I would ask you, please, for a little silence in the Chamber.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u2	Thank you, Madam President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Today I appear before you to detail the budgets planned for the Ministry of Science and Innovation for the year 2023. The general budgets of the State for the coming year and those of my ministry have as their main objective social justice and economic efficiency and are designed around two fundamental axes: the strengthening of the welfare state and inclusive economic growth. The accounts I stand for today in this rostrum align with these objectives because we are certain that science and innovation are levers of growth and progress for our country. We present budgets that contribute to the welfare state by strengthening the public science system with the clear will to help improve the life of citizens, to strengthen research in order to seek solutions and solve the problems of our fellow citizens. And we also present budgets that promote inclusive economic growth. With the new science law we design an updated regulatory framework that is consistent with the current needs of science and innovation and with the current draft budgets we fund the development of the law and those vital ministerial projects to address the future and to foster a new productive model that faces the challenges: the development of clean green energies and their storage, health and cutting-edge therapies, aerospace research and developments, innovative companies and public procurement, the fight against climate emergency and fires, and all this from a system that guarantees real equality between men and women also in science and innovation. Ladies and gentlemen, I should like to turn to the most relevant issues in my department's budget. First of all, I would like to stress that we are faced with the most important budget in R & D+i in the history of our country. For the third consecutive year we increased the funding of the ministry, reaching 3991 million euros. This means that we have doubled the budget in science and innovation in just three years and that we are in the antipodes of the budgets of this department during the governments of the People's Party, which fell by more than 30% and did not recover after the crisis. This is the chart, gentlemen. [[Displays a graph]] With the budgets I am presenting to you today, we are above what marks the path of compliance with the new science law with regard to the target of 1.25% of public R & D funding for 2030. In addition, of the three budgets that we have presented, this is the one that is growing considerably, without taking into account the recovery funds, with an own growth of the national budget of 20%. And this, ladies and gentlemen, is only the contribution to that challenge of reaching 3% of the GDP of the General Administration of the State. We are therefore also serving as an engine for different administrations, such as the autonomous communities, and also for private enterprise. I am not going to detail each and every chapter of the budget, but I would like to highlight some of the actions in two areas that are very relevant: one is human capital and the other is the new strategic projects. At a time of uncertainty and lack of resources, there is one that is inexhaustible and unlimited that we must take care of. I'm not talking about the sun, I'm talking about the human capital, the talent of our researchers. With these budgets we shape one of the objectives that we set ourselves with the law, equip the system with a stable and predictable itinerary for the career and all stages of our research staff. On the one hand, we have approved the largest public offer of employment in science and innovation over the past fifteen years, and in the new budget, research staff will again be regarded, as in the previous two, as priority staff for replacement rates; essential staff, at the same level as education, health and state security forces and bodies. [[applause]] Thus, with a replacement rate of 120 %, for example, every time ten professionals retire, twelve new professionals are incorporated into the public system. Only in the coming year will we be able to incorporate 1983 places of researchers and technicians in the OPI, which will mean renewing more than 12% of the staff of our public science system. In addition, we will strengthen support for the recruitment of young researchers and launch new actions to attract those who had to leave in the last decade. In total, between what remains of this year and 2023, we have convened 3850 places for young researchers predoctoral and postdoctoral. In the case of young pre-doctoral students, we will gradually increase their salary to 75% of the reference salary in all contract annuities, and we will do so over the next four years. In addition, with the increase that will take place this year with the government of Pedro Sánchez and thanks to the approval in 2019 of the Staff Regulations for Training Investigators, we have already revalued their salary by 20%. And also, in order to avoid discontinuities in contracts, we have merged for the first time the call of predoctoral with that of knowledge generation, thus responding to one of the most relevant historical demands, which is not to leave any stage without contract and without contractual coverage for our young people. In addition to these improvements are the new rights approved in the new law on science, compensation for all pre- and post-doctoral contracts at the time of their completion. As you know, this has been one of the great achievements of the new law and it has been applied since its entry into force on 7 September. Therefore, as of 7 September, all new contracts and all contracts that were active are already receiving this contract termination compensation. [[Applauds]] As far as postdoctoral researchers are concerned, in 2023 we will take out again the research consolidation call, which will provide each of these researchers 200 000 euros for their scientific activity and allow them to be incorporated in research centres and universities on a stable basis. In total, with the two calls, this year and next year, 750 researchers will benefit in the consolidation phase. Secondly, we are going to launch for the first time a call to attract high-level researchers, with €40 million. In addition, as is the law, we will improve incentives for the results of the research staff, as experience in the foreigner will be recognised in the five-year period and in the six-year period of transfer. With all these measures we have developed a very significant part of the Plan of attraction and retention of scientific and innovative talent, which we presented and approved in June of this year and whose main objective is to promote the return, retention and attraction of scientific talent to our country. [[Applauds]] And as important as human capital are the projects with which to deal strategically with the future of our country. We want to enhance the entrepreneurial character of our State by promoting initiatives that contribute to the transformation of our country's economy and promote inclusive economic growth, and one such example is the PERTE. In 2023 we will meet the annualities of the calls proposed by our ministry in the rest of the PERTE coordinated with other ministries, such as the PERTE of renewable energies, the Agri-Food PART or the PERTE chip, in which we contribute 835 million euros, while the contribution of the PERTE that we lead is considerably expanded: on the one hand, the PERTE for avant-garde health will increase its public contribution from 982 million euros to 1500 million euros, and on the other, in the Aerospatial PERTE the increased contribution is 350 million euros. Also, as we approved two weeks ago in the Council of Ministers and I will confirm this afternoon in Paris at the ministerial council of the European Space Agency, we will increase Spain's annual contribution to this organisation, so that we move from the current 250 million to the 300 million euros. In total, since 2019 we have increased Spain's contribution to ESA by 50% and now we are the fourth country in the European Union to contribute to it. And I would like to recall the impact of this quota, because, beyond the scientific projects to which we contribute with it - for example, to the Copernicus project, with which we can observe the Earth and with which we were able to predict the volcanic eruption - there is a return georg quota that is very interesting. Thanks to the contribution of our State, this contribution returns to our country in the form of contracts and opportunities for the companies that are in our country and, therefore, for the Spanish companies, so that this contribution to ESA will contribute to a greater growth of the aerospace sector in our country. We will also create two new research bodies: we will launch the new Spanish space agency and the new national Vulcanology Centre will also be created, fulfilling the commitment of the President of the Government to the Canary Islands. In addition, we will launch the innovative public procurement programme for fire prevention, whose objective is to acquire prototypes of first technologically innovative products or services that meet public needs to combat this threat, which is increasingly present today because of climate change. Finally, we are going to launch the call for issues, but this year we have called them transmissions, because it is a call for issues aimed at the transfer of knowledge in which the academic and business world will have to present together, consortia for the resolution of the challenges of the future. And this new call is endowed with 150 million euros. As you know, knowledge transfer is one of the great challenges to change this country's productive model, and that is why in the coming weeks we will present the Knowledge Transfer Plan, endowed with €12 billion, which is based on the budgets of 2023. As you can see, we have a determined commitment to contribute from science and innovation to inclusive economic growth and to change the productive model of our country, repairing people. During the process of the law of science some want to drag us into having to choose between improving workers' conditions or ensuring the stability and excellence of research centres. We always defend and continue to defend that they are compatible and now we do so from the facts and tangible politics. [[Applauds]] Thanks to the new law, the indefinite contract is already the default contract for research staff, also in projects financed from European funds. Moreover, thanks to these new budgets, we will strengthen the funding of research groups and centres of excellence. On the one hand, and as we committed ourselves, we are going to increase the indirect costs by 21%, which the law says to 25% of research projects to strengthen the institutions. And this increase will be made without reducing the direct costs of researchers, i.e. by increasing the funding of calls. In addition, we will move towards a new, less bureaucratic and more flexible funding model, and in this regard announced that with these budgets we will launch a pilot baseline funding project for the research groups of excellence in our country. [[Applauds]] President, ladies and gentlemen, I shall conclude. Before concluding, I would like to thank all the parliamentary groups who have shown their willingness to support these budgets. Four years ago, we found ourselves in a system of elusive science, more focused on survival than in the future; a public policy on science and innovation subject to urgency and on the basis of restrictive budgets and cuts. At this time we have addressed many of the misadjustments, acting on the precariousness of science workers and designing a new legislative framework. There is a long way to go and these changes take time to work out, but we are convinced that in the near future we will see how these years were the turning point that our science system needed. In fact, we are already seeing the results; among the new social security affiliations, the sector that has grown the most since 2020 in number of affiliates is that of computer science and telecommunications, i.e. the innovation sector, and the third sector that is growing the most is that of scientific and technical activities. If we add the two together, one in four new jobs that are being created in our country are in the field of science and innovation. Therefore, we have gone from expelling our talent to recruiting. [[Applauds]] This will be our legacy and this will be our greatest contribution to the future. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]

In Spain there is no national center of R+D+i on dinosaur paleontology, although there is a history of paleontological theme in the case of the National Center of Research on Human Evolution, CENIEH, which so excellent results is giving, in Burgos. The province of Teruel, for many aspects, such as the variety of geological ages, number of deposits and diversity of fossils, is the most suitable place to develop this center of dinosaur paleontology. In Teruel almost half of the species of all new dinosaurs discovered in Spain have been found and studied, including the largest dinosaur in Europe, about thirty meters long, called Turiasaurus, the Turia lizard or Teruel. The province also stands out because since 2001 there has been a paleontological park known as Dinópolis. This Centre, as well as progressing in fossil research at both national and international levels, is a true engine of tourism and socio-economic development in much of the territory. In other words, Teruel would be the right place for the creation of this national research centre in dinosaur paleontology. Similarly, I would like to recall a new institution that the budgets are going to finance, the Spanish Space Agency. Madam Minister, you know that we have worked to promote your creation and improve your definition through the law of science and commissions, and you certainly remember that we are belligerent so that the headquarters of a scientific institution of this state can be located according to your capacity to improve territorial cohesion. We are still in time, the ministry should be sensitive to the position we have put before it. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u22	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear to outline the budgets of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the European Union and Cooperation for the year 2023, which are aligned with our priorities for the coming year. In a convulsive, complex and volatile international context, such as the current one, the work of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation is fundamental, implementing a European foreign and cooperation policy that has and will continue to have the defence of the Spaniards and their interests and the projection of their values as a top priority, because the vaccines we put ourselves to protect ourselves against the COVID-19, the currency we use or our security can only be guaranteed to work together with our partners, friends and allies in the world. That is why firm, determined and committed external action is needed to protect our citizens, our families, our businesses, and to project our values into the world. For this reason, the budget of the department that I am running for 2023 amounts to EUR 2009 million, an increase of 14.26% compared to 2022, that is, EUR 250.73 million more in absolute terms to carry out a foreign policy at the service of the Spanish citizens and to deepen the modernization of the foreign service of Spain; all this in order to continue working on the priority lines of our external action. First of all, peace and security, in order to bring peace back to Europe as soon as possible and thus guarantee the security of the Spaniards, betting on multilateralism and the United Nations to solve the global problems we face. Secondly, to maintain our commitment to official development assistance and cooperation in addressing the food crisis, the energy crisis and the humanitarian crisis that are currently devastating the world. Thirdly, we are strongly projected and led Europe during our Presidency of the European Union in the second half of next year. Fourthly, to promote our common language and our co-official languages and our culture in the world. Finally, to ensure the care and protection of almost three million Spaniards living abroad. Ladies and gentlemen, Russia's aggression against Ukraine has shown us that we must redouble our efforts to protect world peace and security, which is an absolute priority of Spain's external action. This year we have had the responsibility of hosting a NATO summit that was historic because of the importance of what was agreed in the Madrid Strategic Concept and for the success of its organization by Spain. In that regard, we will continue to work with our partners within the Alliance and the European Union to protect peace. Spain wishes to remain a constructive actor in the maintenance and pursuit of international peace and security and, therefore, we are promoting our candidature to the United Nations Security Council as a non-permanent member for the period 20312032, which will be our sixth term. In addition to an illegal, unjust and unjustified aggression that is causing thousands of victims and a great deal of suffering to the civilian population, Russia's aggression against Ukraine has broken the pillars of the international order that illuminated the fall of the Berlin Wall. We are at a defining moment for Europe in which two models are faced, that of European construction, that of tolerance and democracy, and the authoritarian model of Vladimir Putin. Putin's aggression against Ukraine has also led to a food and energy crisis that adds to other challenges already present, such as climate change or digital transformation. We must deal with all this by cooperating multilaterally, because only by doing things well outside will it be good for the Spaniards within our borders. Multilateralism is key to addressing these global challenges and, therefore, in these general state budgets we maintain Spain's commitment to multilateralism and the United Nations, paying special attention to actions in defence of human rights with a total credit of €505.59 million, of which €39.23 million will be earmarked for voluntary contributions to international agencies and €364 million will continue to support the work of United Nations agencies and fulfil our commitments under the 2030 Agenda. Spain's commitment to multilateralism is also exemplified by the growing presence of the United Nations system in our own country, in our territory. Over the past year, we have continued to work to expand the United Nations Information and Communications Technology Centre in Quart de Poblet, Valencia, with new facilities that can accommodate the digital technology departments of Unicef and the International Organization for Migration. The United Nations Counter-Terrorism Centre has also recently opened a new headquarters in Madrid, with which we will collaborate in the implementation of counter-terrorism projects. Barcelona will host the Giga Technology Centre, the UN program to digitally connect all schools in the world, and last October 31st we inaugurated in Bilbao the headquarters of the Secretariat of the 2030 Local Coalition, the United Nations agency in charge of coordinating the implementation of the Sustainable Development Goals at the levels closest to citizens. But the food crisis, the energy crisis and the climate crisis remain pressing challenges, probably the most pressing in the world today. We must take urgent measures to minimize its impact and to prevent it from leading to serious humanitarian crises. Addressing these crises through international cooperation is, therefore, and will continue to be, a priority for our external action in the next budget. The budgets for 2023 reflect the largest increase in official development assistance in our country since 2011, reaching 4419.15 million euros. In particular, the ministry I run will contribute to official development aid in 2023 with EUR 1118.81 million; this represents an increase of EUR 206 million and 23% more than the general state budgets of 2022. This commitment to international cooperation for sustainable development is a constant sign of the identity of this Government. Official development aid has thus risen to 0.28% in 2022 and will reach 0.34% of gross national income in 2023, following a path of sustained growth. As I said before, for the Spaniards to go well inside we have to do things well outside, much more in the current international context, because it is also necessary for our neighbours, our partners and our friends in the world to go well, since international cooperation is investment in the well-being of the world that results in investment in the well-being of the Spaniards. That is why in 2023 we will continue to be donors to institutions such as FAO, IFAD and the World Food Programme. At the summit on food security, which Spain chaired on the margins of the last General Assembly of the United Nations, we announced that we have allocated a total of 236.5 million euros for various projects in the field of food security over the next three years. Spain will also continue to promote health as a global public good and universal access to the COVID-19 vaccine, which is far from being eradicated in many countries around the world. For this reason, over the next three years we have allocated over €237 million to the health sector, of which $130 million will go to the Global Fund to Fight AIDS, Tuberculosis and Malaria. In addition, next year we will continue to support the people and Government of Ukraine in their defence of peace, freedom, territorial integrity and sovereignty by sending humanitarian aid. Earlier this month, I delivered thirty ambulances equipped with emergency consultation and treatment materials to girls, adolescents and pregnant women in Kiev, and to attend childbirth. Last Friday fourteen generators donated by Spain went out to Ukraine through its cooperation agency, AECID, to alleviate the needs created by attacks on that country's energy infrastructure. Actions like this, which have been many during the war, will be supported by the budget of our cooperation. Ladies and gentlemen, Spain is playing an active role in proposing fair responses to the global crisis, acting with solidarity in Europe and internationally. This leadership of Spain will be projected with special force during the Spanish Presidency of the European Union in the second half of next year. The Spanish Presidency will be one of the most important moments in our foreign policy in many years. We held the previous Presidency in 2010 and it will not be until at least 13 years after we re-chair the Council of the European Union. Spain's European policy is not separate - you know perfectly well - from our internal policy, and that is precisely why our Presidency must be a country project. The Spanish Presidency will not only be important for the Spanish, it will also be important for the Europeans. Europe today more than ever needs Spanish leadership, strong leadership and ambitious momentum, if we are to live up to the challenges we face and successfully face them. The Spaniards can and must offer that leadership; that is what is expected of us in our next presidency. We are one of the most European societies on the continent. We are one of the countries with the highest demographic and economic weight in the Union and, therefore, the ministry I lead has budgeted a total of 19.61 million euros to finance staff costs and costs of organizing conferences and meetings, and to adapt spaces and investments for the organization of the sixty-two meetings planned by the ministry I lead. In 2023 we will also continue to work for the impetus of our culture, our common language and our co-official languages, all using our common heritage as a factor for economic growth and also for competitiveness and international influence. The Cervantes Institute, a great reference in this task, will have a budget of 167.5 million euros - which is 4% more than the previous year - that will be destined to the improvement of the network of centers, the adaptation of the expenses to the levels of IPC and to cover the expenses necessary to maintain and increase the levels of own income and to cover the costs deriving from the new centers in Los Angeles and Seoul and the Global Observatory of Spanish that will be based in La Rioja, as well as to put into operation the new extension in Bangalore dependent on the already existing center in New Delhi. Also, through the Secretariat of State for Ibero-America and the Caribbean and Spanish in the World, €200,000 will be allocated for the convening of a line of grants for activities of interest for the promotion and expansion of Spanish beyond our borders. Finally, in this very complex world, we must ensure the care and protection of the nearly three million compatriots living outside our borders. That is why we will strengthen our network of Spanish embassies and consulates in the world. Our presence and activity abroad will be allocated 45% of the budget, i.e. EUR 899 million, with the aim of providing good service and protecting our citizens living outside. The consular action has an appropriation of EUR 3.37 million, of which EUR 2.3 million is earmarked for aid for the protection of Spaniards abroad, i.e. aid to welfare institutions, such as subsistence and extraordinary economic aid; aid to Spaniards deprived of their liberty in foreign prisons; and aid to manage the potential repatriations of Spaniards, with special follow-up in the case of repatriations of victims of gender-based violence. In addition, these budgets incorporate 49.25 million euros of the European Plan for Recovery, Transformation and Resilience to accelerate the integral digital transformation of our consular service and give impetus to the digital teaching of Spanish abroad. The strengthening of the consular service will also serve to respond to the many needs arising from the repeal of the requested vote and the adoption of the Democratic Memory Act, which will allow children and grandchildren of Spanish exiles to opt for Spanish nationality. Ladies and gentlemen, in the development of our European foreign and cooperation policy, the human team of the ministry I lead is fundamental. In 2023 we will have 5961 public employees, of which 4624 are destined abroad and 1337 in central services. Staff costs will be increased by 7.73 per cent over the previous year, which will allow the needs to be adjusted to economic reality and the effects of inflation to be mitigated. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, we are presenting a draft budget to meet the priorities of Spain's external action with a foreign policy that responds to the great current challenges and the international context - the most convulsed since the fall of the Berlin wall - marked by the war in Ukraine and its economic and social consequences that have led to a global crisis. It is a foreign policy that is strongly committed to inclusive, effective and peace-building multilateralism; international cooperation to provide fair responses to these crises; promoting peace along with security; continuing to promote the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals; contributing to food security; protecting human rights; promoting an inclusive ecological and digital transition; and strengthening Spain's presence in the United Nations system. All of this being very aware that we will have to lead the European Union next year since its presidency. In short, these budgets aim to support a foreign policy which has as its main objective the protection and defence of the interests of the Spaniards abroad and of the Europeans during our Presidency, which will also benefit us within our borders. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

This historic opportunity for recovery funds cannot be missed, let alone lost only by the shyness of a Government in making decisions about important reforms that are pending. It is of no use for the Government to presume laws passed in this House if those laws do not address the real problems of the Spanish people and do not undertake the necessary reforms for our country to grow and be competitive in the twenty-first century. That is why we have tabled a number of amendments to this effect, so that the government has to respect the European guidelines when it comes to using European funds. These funds come with conditions and, if they are not complied with, the European Union can paralyse shipments or'command the return of such funds. We do not want Spain to be seen as a country that does not comply with its European commitments simply, as I say, because of the shyness of the government to solve and carry out structural reforms. In addition, we have proposed to strengthen the government's accountability for the use of these European funds, forcing Parliament to be informed of any changes in the allocation of these funds. In this sense, the liberals understand the importance of foreign and European affairs to Spain's reputation in the world. In order to carry out the work of this ministry, we in Citizens have also tabled several amendments aimed at improving our foreign service and opening the doors of the Administration to the first talent we have in our country. For example, we have proposed a specific budget of almost EUR 1 million for the creation of a programme of paid practices for professionals. This programme, to which we have baptized Margarita Salaverría in honour of the first Spanish diplomatic woman, would allow professionals who have recently graduated or experienced in the private sector also to gain experience in the administration through a stay in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation or in the AECID. This stay is also complementary to another in the network of Spanish embassies and consulates around the world or in the technical cooperation offices of AECID in other developing countries in which Spanish cooperation operates. In short, it is an opportunity to take advantage of the talent of young people in our country and also to break the unacceptable practice of the Administration to offer unpaid practices and thus prevent many middle-class families from being able to afford this training for their children. We have also tabled an amendment to allocate EUR 300,000 to accompany Spanish professionals in their access to the institutions of the European Union. In a year when Spain holds the rotating presidency of the Council of the European Union, we believe that it is necessary, more necessary than ever, to give the Spaniards these positions of relevance and decision-making in Brussels and to give them the importance they deserve; it is important, of course, that other countries whose economic and demographic weight in the European Union is smaller than that of Spain, such as the Netherlands or Poland, have been able to play better and encourage their nationals to reach positions of responsibility. With this budget we hope that we can accompany our professionals, help them to make contacts and prepare for the European opposition, so that Spain will be better represented in the institutions of the European Union in Brussels and in the various executive agencies. We have also tabled another series of amendments - strikingly, moreover, we have done so in other general budgets of the State - which have not even wanted to review, and they do not because, obviously, they do not come from the hands of their partners, and that is not good for our country. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]

The proposed budgets for the year 2023 is in this same sense a real step forward if we also consider the perfect link between the own funds and the funds derived from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. Budgeted resources for public justice policy amount to EUR 2291 million in the draft budget. I am advancing an easy criticism, which is also an unfair criticism. Of course, this year the amount earmarked for European funds has fallen, because at this point, when we are still in 2022, we have fulfilled practically all the transfers to the autonomous communities for the year 2022, but also for the year 2023. We've moved on. The co-governance mechanisms operate, and in the case of the Ministry of Justice with the absolute support of the autonomous communities with transferred competences - twelve of the seventeen, as you know - where the planning for the years 2022 and 2023 was agreed at the meeting of the Sectoral Conference of Administration of Justice on 25 March last and credits amounting to EUR 179.2 million were transferred. Of these, appropriations amounting to EUR 161.3 million were transferred in June this year, which is 90% of the agreed amount, and that is why only the outstanding amount for the year 2023 remains to be transferred, the remaining 10% amounting to approximately EUR 17.9 million. This is why only 57.5 million are collected as an annuality planned for 2023 under European funds. If we eliminate, therefore, the effect of European funds, in case anyone were tempted in this debate, public justice policy receives a grant in the 2023 project of 2233.82 million euros, that is, 160 million more than for this year we are in. We are therefore facing a budget increase of 7.7%. Of this overall amount of public justice policy, the funds allocated to the Ministry of Justice, including the Centre for Legal Studies, amount to EUR 2155.48 million, an increase of EUR 158 million, 7.9%. It is essential to mention the funds allocated to the General Council of the Judiciary within the overall justice policy, which reach 78.33 million euros, which also implies an increase of almost 3%. The transformation that we are going to achieve with these funds is going to be a turning point in the consideration of justice by citizens and we are going to act on two fundamental axes: universal accessibility and efficiency. Firstly, in terms of accessibility, accessibility is free justice; it means improving offices for victim assistance and care. They are also the judicial offices in the municipalities and the commitment to patent and present justice throughout the territory, regardless of the size of the localities, regardless of the size of their population, as a service more for the citizenry and with special attention to the areas with less population density. As I said, it is a commitment to territorial cohesion through social cohesion and digital cohesion. Imagine, ladies and gentlemen, what it will mean in terms of accompanying citizens to have municipal judicial offices for the elderly, for persons with disabilities, for economically vulnerable persons, for persons who by any other kind of personal or social circumstance do not have those technological means available, to avoid digital gaps, territorial gaps, generational gaps, gaps of any kind. In addition, they will also bring about improvements in the lives of people and the functioning of the courts in the territories managed by the autonomous governments, which, as I have already said, are those twelve autonomous communities. We are also focused on those who work in the Public Justice Service to make their conditions more favourable: investment in digitisation, which will save procedures that are now almost handcrafted, and also the possibility of teleworking when possible. And there is a clear bet in these budgets also for young people, for their vocation to impart justice, a bet for their talent, that they see no barriers or obstacles to fulfill this desire for socio-economic causes or other personal circumstances or for the place in which they live. Consequently, for the second year in a row, this Government is committed to improving access to judicial and prosecutorial careers, to the legal profession of the Administration of Justice and to the State Advocate with a strengthened scholarship system and also to online training. The Center for Legal Studies is also committed to training with the dimension of digital transformation in which we are immersed not only in Spain, but throughout the world. Moreover, it must be stressed that legal professions cannot be alien to this process of transformation; they must be a driving force and seek legal and strategic solutions to deal with it. Secondly, resource management will also be based on improved efficiency. The evolution of the more than 3600 Courts of First Instance and Instruction that we have in our territory towards the 431 Courts of Justice, sharing the resources of the Justice Offices. Attention, because no judicial party, no head of judicial party in Spain will be left without its Court of Justice. Where there are now courts in all party heads, in all judicial parties, there will also be a Court of Justice. We will promote the culture of the agreement through the training and the laws that are in place in parliamentary office, which they know as well as I do, and we will promote the holding of telematic judgments and remote statements or the possibility of carrying out all kinds of usual procedures for citizens from the offices of Justice in the municipality or even from their own smartphones, from their electronic elements, putting justice by hand, literally, of citizens. For these two axes, the different budget chapters are broken down. Chapter 1, staff costs, exceeding EUR 1785 million, for staff receiving their salaries from the budget of the Ministry of Justice. This chapter accounts for 77,5 % of the ministry's budget and it is clear that it has grown by EUR 139 million, i.e. 8,4 %. This includes the introduction of the new model of judicial office; the creation of fifty-four mediation units, which represent a firm step in the commitment to that culture of the agreement; the creation of twenty-seven administrative units to assist victims; the creation of seventy judicial units; the expansion of the Office of the Prosecutor in seventy places; the creation of places for the new central register of royal titles; the upgrading to the category of magistrate in five judicial parties; new incorporations in the field of central services and the State Advocate and with more than 77 million euros the overall salary increase for public employees. And I would also like to refer to the lawyers and lawyers of the Justice Administration, fundamental agents in the proceedings, fundamental agents in our judicial system, whose salaries have received substantial improvements from the ministry in this year 2022 with the new royal decree of salaries that was promulgated in April, retroactively, and also in the field of interventions in entries and records, as well as the replacements made by these professionals. In this way, we note the considerable increase that has been made in this legislature in the creation of judicial units and bodies, which have already begun by my predecessors, Minister Delgado and Minister Campo. In 2019, seventy-five new judicial units; in 2020, thirty-three and three places of magistrates and in 2021, fifty-six and four places of territorial secondment judge. In 2022, the establishment of the seventy judicial units was under way and budget allocations for the establishment of another seventy judicial units were foreseen for the year 2023. And I cannot leave without mentioning the push that has also been given to the expansion of the organizational staff of the Prosecutor's Office. In 2019, eighty places were created; in 2021, in the general budgets, forty places were supposed to be created, and in May 2022, sixty places were created instead of the forty initially budgeted, and in these budgets for the financial year 2023 we provide for the creation of seventy other places. On the other hand, since 2019 a considerable effort has also been made to call for places for judicial and prosecutorial careers: 1040 places convened since 2019 and another 200 that are planned for 2023. Chapter 2, current expenditure on goods and services, remains virtually constant, reaching EUR 146.50 million, while Chapter 3, financial expenditure, is increased by EUR 0.33 million, reaching EUR 0.66 million. In Chapter 4, current transfers, if we discount the effect of European funds, the item increases by 3.5 million euros - 5.2% increase -, destined basically for the transfer that is made for the financing of the Center of Legal Studies. Chapter 6, real investment, increases by EUR 38.58 million over the budget of 2022, i.e. an increase of 16%, to EUR 279,8 million. Of these, more than 53 million are earmarked for infrastructure,equipment and buildings, almost 139 for technological modernisation and 87.64 for digital justice transformation and the energy transition from headquarters. By 2023 the construction projects of the new buildings of the courts of Talavera de la Reina, Tomelloso, Illescas, Cartagena, Molina de Segura, Cáceres, Navalmoral de la Mata, Valladolid, Ibiza and Ciudadela are planned. In addition, the works of the Supreme Court and the legal library of the Ministry of Justice, the new buildings of the courts of Torrijos, Lorca and Manacor, the new offices of the Institute of Legal Medicine of Toledo and the National Institute of Toxicology and Forensic Sciences in Two Sisters, the rehabilitation of the Provincial Court of Salamanca and the court building of Ocaña will take place. And the works already begun, such as those of the new court building in Badajoz, will continue. Finally, it is planned to complete the rehabilitation of the Manzanares and Puertollano courthouses building and the rehabilitation of the Toledo Palace of Justice. Chapter 7, capital transfers, decreases EUR 108.67 million. As I mentioned at the beginning of my speech, this decrease is precisely due to the good planning for the years 2022 and 2023, carried out in co-government with the autonomous communities, from the funds of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, so that 90% of what was agreed on in appropriations in 2022 has already been transferred and only 10% of what was planned, as I said before. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, Chapter 8, financial assets, maintains the same allocations as in 2022, about EUR 620,000. In relation to the public bodies attached to the Ministry, the General Judicial Mutuality and the Centre for Legal Studies, the expenditure budget for the latter reaches 19,62 million euros, an increase of 3,4 million euros, that is, more than 20% regarding the budget approved for the year 2022. I would like to highlight here the credit for scholarships in the preparation of oppositions to judicial and prosecutorial careers, to the superior body of lawyers and lawyers of the Administration of Justice and to the State Advocate. This credit is doubled to EUR 3,24 million in order to promote equality in access to these excellence careers. The 2022 scholarship programme allowed for a total of 245 scholarships, amounting to EUR 1 600 000, and by 2023 we intend to double the amount allocated to these scholarships, which means strongly supporting this commitment for equal opportunities and also for the young vocation in legal careers. With regard to the General Judicial Mutuality, its budget for 2023 amounts to EUR 128.17 million, an increase of 2.5% over the previous year and with particular attention to both the economic benefits and the health care of administrative mutualism. I would also like to refer to the budget of the Spanish Data Protection Agency, which must face the increase in its functions. Its Chapter 1 is increased by EUR 1.72 million, 17.38 %, up to EUR 11.60 million of the Agency's allocation, an important step towards carrying out its very important functions in an appropriate and efficient manner. By increasing Chapter 2 by 2.02%, up to €5.47 million, it will face up to its functions in relation to e-government and also to the Agency's own administrative management systems. And in Chapter 6, the 7.54% increase, up to EUR 998 350, will improve electronic headquarters and automation in the claims management process, In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, governance, social cohesion, territorial cohesion, digital cohesion, accessibility, equal opportunities and efficiency. These are the key words in section 13 of the Ministry of Justice in the Draft General State Budgets for the year 2023. And I want to tell you, although we will have the right time, that the budget implementation for 2021 was excellent, but that 2022 is going to go further along that path and with this project for 2023, along with the normative projects that you are currently working on, the efficiency laws, I am sure, ladies and gentlemen, that together we will get our justice to have a huge breakthrough in our country. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u64	Good morning. Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, before we begin to set out section 14 of the Draft Law on General State Budgets for the year 2023, I would like to stress that when we talk about investing in defence we not only refer to transforming and modernising the capabilities of our Armed Forces, we propose investing in peace, security, freedom and democratic values. Investing in defence is also supporting the Spanish business fabric by strengthening the industrial and technological defence base that acts as a driver of economic growth and employment generation. We are faced with a budgetary lever that allows us to support research, development and innovation, R & D+i, essential elements for the growth and prosperity of 21st century Spain. We are going to deal with a wide variety of expenses, but let me stress that all the budgetary effort we have made would not make sense without a human capital of enormous professional capacity, as are the men and women of our Armed Forces. For us to invest in defence is to further improve the training and preparation of our military and, of course, to improve their working conditions, to intensify reconciliation and social protection, to increase gender equality policies and to enable them to increase their operational training to carry out their work, of which we are so proud. Ladies and gentlemen, since last February, the international context in which we find ourselves has been marked by the illegal, illegitimate and cruel war that began Putin, solely responsible for the current situation, by ordering the unjustified Russian invasion of Ukraine. From here I would like to take advantage of my intervention to pay tribute and witness to the heroic Ukrainian people and our utmost support to the Government of Ukraine in its fight against the invasion and for the maintenance of its territorial integrity and independence. [[Applauds]] This war, which has destroyed international peace and stability, poses a threat to the democratic values we share with our partners and allies in the European Union, NATO, and is affecting international trade, generating famine for millions of citizens throughout the world, especially in Africa. I would remind you that at the NATO Summit held in Madrid last June, we agreed to strengthen the stance of deterrence and defence, as well as to promote innovation and increase defence investment in emerging and disruptive technologies to maintain the necessary technological advantage that will undoubtedly intensify the necessary policies of deterrence to potential aggressors. That is why it is so important at this time to express also the support to our Spanish soldiers and to the members of the CNI who, at this time, within the framework of NATO, are in Latvia, Bulgaria, Braila - Romania - and are also in the Mediterranean Sea, just under the umbrella of NATO, helping to highlight the character of Spain as a serious, responsible and reliable ally. [[Applauds]] On the whole, the budgets in section 14 that I am presenting today are realistic and expansive budgets that respond to the international scene. The budget for Section 14 for the year 2023 amounts to EUR 12,825 million, which represents an increase in investment in defence policies of 25.8% over the year 2022. This increase also affects the autonomous bodies under the Ministry of Defence and the National Intelligence Centre. The appropriations allocated to the National Intelligence Centre are increased by 4.6% over the budgets of 2022. With regard to staff policies, the total appropriations allocated to Chapter 1, staff costs, amount to EUR 5375.48 million. These appropriations, representing 41.9% of the department's total budget, represent an increase of EUR 313.64 million, 6.20% more than the 2022 budget. This growth in spending will consolidate the wage improvements approved in the years 2020 and 2021 and address the authorized remuneration of public employees in the year 2023, which, together with maintaining the replacement rate by 120 %, will ensure the human resource necessary to ensure the operationality of our Armed Forces. Continuing these personnel policies, the Ministry of Defence will increase its budgetary effort in the area of training and professional development. We will allocate EUR 455.5 million to the education and training of the personnel of the Armed Forces. We bet on a unique and quality teaching model, innovative and personalized. As I said before, the war in Ukraine has shown, alongside the pandemic, the enormous role played by military health. That is why we will allocate EUR 178.2 million to hospital care in the Armed Forces, an increase of 11.24% over 2022. It is very important to take into account this provision of health care which, in addition, we saw and demonstrated as help and collaboration with society when we lived in the COVID, the pandemic and the great work that our Armed Forces did, which we will never sufficiently thank to have been with the elderly, with those who needed it, even by watching over the dead. At this point, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to make a special mention of the work being done by the military hospital in Zaragoza. At this time, we have brought in 32 Ukrainian soldiers with terrible amputations and damage from the war, and there they are receiving all kinds of assistance and treatment from all the personnel of that military hospital in Zaragoza, and I would also like to highlight the help of the Government of Aragon. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, the items devoted to Chapter 2, current expenditure on goods and services, in the year 2023, amount to 1026.5 million, an increase of 117.47 million euros; this represents an increase of 12.92 % over the previous year. This increase will enable us to cope with the strengthening of the deterrence and defence stance agreed upon by all NATO allies, strengthening the preparation of force and improving the levels of instruction, training and availability. Investment will also be made in the recovery of infrastructure and other facilities with an impact on the quality of life of the troops and mariners and in the improvement of communication systems. For its part, the appropriations in Chapter 4, current transfers, amount to EUR 471.74 million, an increase of 3.57 % compared to this year's budget, an amount that will be used to strengthen the cybersecurity of the National Intelligence Centre, as well as the teaching and training of our staff in digital skills. Undoubtedly, a new scenario in the defense in which Spain should not stay behind. As regards Chapter 6, dedicated to real investment, the appropriations amount to EUR 5868.93 million. They represent 45.75 % of the Ministry of Defence's budget. This chapter, with an increase of EUR 2221.61 million, 60.91% more than last year, is the one that is increasing the most. Within this chapter, the allocation for special modernisation programmes amounts to EUR 4902 million, with an increase of EUR 2053.71 million; this is an increase of 72,11% over 2022. Thanks to this budget allocation, we will be able to continue, and I think it is important, the 20 ongoing programmes for the modernization of our Armed Forces, as well as to devote 1296 million to starting 13 new programmes. This remarkable increase in investment will have an important driver for the defence industry on economic development, which will also translate into an impetus for R & D+i. As a result, 22 667 jobs will be created directly or indirectly, and I think it is important to point out that there are 22 667 jobs here. Of course, and this is not a minor issue, our international reputation in the field of the defence industry is prestigious, as has been demonstrated by Navantia's contract to participate in the manufacture of three ships for the British Navy. It is obvious that no one escapes that these programmes, in addition to promoting the modernization of our armed forces so much needed, involve this increase in jobs and, moreover, give enormous vitality to certain regions. The construction of our frigates F 110 is done in the region of Ferrol, our new underwater intervention ship BAM-IS is done in the Bay of Cadiz and the construction of the submarine S80 is being done in Cartagena. It is job creation. He also mentioned the A400M programmes in Seville, which will create jobs in that city and in the surrounding area, and the combat vehicle on 8x8 wheels, which is creating jobs in Asturias, Guipúzcoa and Madrid. This could be followed by the more than thirty programmes that will be developed throughout the national territory. Thanks to them, we also make a commitment to transatlantic cooperation and the development of the common European security and defence policy, increasing by 55.2% the allocations for permanent structured cooperation projects of the European Union, also known as PESCO. These are, ladies and gentlemen, the fundamental lines of the Ministry of Defence's budgets, budgets that are more necessary than ever; budgets that, with a very strong increase in public spending, consolidate the trend that this government began at the beginning of this legislature. With these budgets we will reach 1.2% of GDP in defence in 2023 and mark the path of the unequivocal commitment to reach 2% of GDP in 2029. Budgets that respond to the challenges arising from the international situation that we live in and show the solidity, the commitment of Spain to our partners and allies in contributing to security, defence and peace in the world. NATO Secretary General Jens Stoltenberg just made a few statements this morning here in Spain highlighting and thanking Spain for its cooperation and participation in supporting Ukraine. Because, as I said before, to support Ukraine is to support peace, freedom and democratic values, and in that we are fully committed; there is Spain and there is the Ministry of Defence. He also said that the Spanish economy is fully committed to this positive impact because the planned investments, as he said, strengthen the productive fabric and drive the industrial and technological base of defence. I should like to conclude by thanking the parliamentary groups for supporting these general state budgets for the year 2023. With them, Spain - I will always say it bluntly - invests in prosperity, social cohesion, peace - yes, peace - freedom and security. As I said at the beginning, I cannot finish my speech without paying tribute to all the men and women of our Armed Forces who are currently deployed on distant continents, working for peace and security in a disinterested, silent and dedicated way of service and surrender, in which I am sure that all the citizens of Spain support us. I also remember all those who, in recent months, have lost their lives in service and those who, at this time, are recovering from the wounds. All of them give us an example of will, commitment, public service and love not only to Spain, but to the citizenship of the world as a whole. That is why, clearly, investing in defence is investing in peace, it is investing in democratic values. That Government was proud of that, as it was proud of the fact that Spain would be placed as a country seriously committed to the search for peace in all international spheres. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u70	Thank you very much, Madam President. Good morning, Minister, ladies and gentlemen. We are debating at this time of the plenary session section 14 of the general budgets of the State, the Defence budget, for the next 2023. This is certainly a unique year, in which there is a substantial increase in the defence budget, around 26%, concentrated especially on certain programmes, which I will comment on below. We welcome the increase and hope that it can continue to be maintained in the coming years in order to achieve the shared objective - not imposed, Mr Rego, but shared - with our allies to invest in defence at least 2% of our gross domestic product, which is equally devoted at least 20% of it to special programmes of main weapons systems, including research, development and innovation. Again, as in previous years, the draft State General Budgets for 2023 is experiencing a significant increase in the real investment section of Chapter 6, in programme 122.A,modernisation, with a total increase of 15.30 %, and, above all, in the 122B, special programmes, with an increase of 72.11 % over 2022. Similarly, as was the case in previous years, with regard to training and support - this year it is growing a little, but in previous years it was even decreasing - the 122M operational expenditure programme is growing by 2.25%, and the 122N, logistical support, another scant 3.05%. Mr Honrubia spoke yesterday, in a horribly angry way - as usual, inducing hatred of our parliamentary group, and which he so criticizes in actions that are produced by desalmados with which we have nothing more or less to see than in the United States of America - of the day of the marmot, that is, of the repetition of our arguments year after year, when year after year there is the same model of definition of the general budgets of the state for defense. In our view - if, with the permission of Mr Honrubia or the left as a whole, we can have our own opinion - the overall growth of the defence budget, to which we welcome, as I have said, should be distributed more proportionately among these four programmes, since it is not complacent to acquire new weapons systems and not to grow proportionally to that investment in acquisitions the training and support sections. On the other hand, training is linked not only to the availability of new weapons systems, but also to the maintenance of effectiveness in the implementation of operational procedures. And within the 122.M program, operating expenses, as was the case last year, the amount allocated to operations abroad remains unchanged, postponing another year the decision to incorporate into the Defence budget the costs of our participation in operations abroad and not leaving them for contingency funds. As far as the salaries of the Armed Forces personnel are concerned, this year the increase in salaries is assumed to be around 3.5 per cent common to officials of the General Administration of the State. In our relationship with the various professional associations of the Armed Forces recognized by the Ministry of Defence, there is a recurring need to undertake a review of these salaries in order to dignify the exercise of the profession and the performance of their duties, in view of the pay gap between the military and other officials of the General Administration who perform tasks of similar gravity, danger and availability. The military complain little and do a lot, and it is up to the political leaders, us, to make the necessary decisions so that this altruism and the surrender that they show, which sometimes represents a singular sacrifice for their families, are adequately compensated. That is why for years, as a result of the low level of purchasing power of the professionals of the Armed Forces in relation to other officials of their level of responsibility, danger and penosity, we have been advocating the allocation of a monthly linear increase per member of the military, regardless of employment, of one hundred euros two years ago and one hundred sixty last year, that this year is necessarily increased by an additional 20 million euros to assimilate the lowest level of the non-commissioned officers to the administrative level 20, being the lowest level of the A2 of the Administration. The total amount of this accumulated increase would be estimated at some EUR 300 million, although this is not considered a final point, but a starting point for undertaking the overall review of the salaries of the military with the aim of reconciling their sacrifices and providing them with access to the benefits necessary for the development of an adequate family life. However, year after year the budget managers - as on the day of Mr Honrubia's marmot, considered the most social in the history of Spain - once again vetoed this proposal because it is not theirs; and not only to us, but also to other groups who presented it. On the other hand, each and every amendment tabled by this parliamentary group aimed at a more balanced and balanced distribution of the budget has been rejected, facilitating the availability by the armies and the Navy of sufficient funds for training and support, absolutely essential to guarantee their operational capacity, an essential parameter to assess the effectiveness of our defence, essential for the maintenance of our security. They have also rejected amendments aimed at compensating local and autonomous administrations for the impact they sometimes experience as a result of absolutely essential exercises for the materialization of the training of our Armed Forces, such as, for example, an amendment concerning the Field of Manoeuvres and Teleno Shooting, in León, which has for years not received the compensation it had previously received to heal the roads altered by the passage of heavy vehicles, specifically artillery, which carry out shooting practices in that field of maneuvers. In conclusion, they also vetoed a proposal, long pursued by various groups and agreed with the Minister of Defence, as Mr Gutiérrez mentioned, for the honorary promotion to the job of lieutenant for a group of non-commissioned officers on the reserve, without any economic cost; quite regrettable all this, indeed. In particular, however, I do not rule out that this proposal will finally be met, given its lack of economic impact. We know that they reject all these amendments tabled by the People's Party simply because they are presented by the People's Party, but we also know that they then implement some of these measures that are proposed to them because they know they are good.

On the one hand, the departure of the Tax Agency, which is increasing by 16%, reaching a record figure of 1232 million, which, as you know, is aimed mainly at combating tax fraud and also at ensuring compliance with tax obligations with personalized attention for each taxpayer. On the other hand, the second transfer is to the Transparency and Good Government Council, which is increasing by 2.7 million, because commitment to democratic quality, accountability and the implementation of some legislative texts requires reinforcement for a body that reports to society on the activities of the Government or other tasks entrusted to it. In chapter 6, real investments, there are 123 million, which means an increase of 6.5% for the acquisition and renovation of computer equipment, the revision of the real estate register, the development of open government in the digital environment, the drive for quality and innovation processes in the public administration and new applications in the Office of Conflict of Interest to make effective the verification that no person who leaves office subsequently engaged in an activity that is not expressly permitted by law. Chapter 7 is endowed with 474 million, 13 more than we had in this budget, and includes transfers of 221 million to autonomous communities for digital modernization projects. Similarly, ladies and gentlemen, there are 142 million for grants to companies located in areas of economic promotion. Funding from autonomous communities and local authorities will be provided, the first, with EUR 15 billion and the second, with EUR 1 billion. Both are the result of the application of the territorial system and, therefore, the question is to consider in the draft budgets only the amounts that the financing model contains because, as you know, with regard to the participation of the entities in the taxes that the State manages is not recorded within the budgets and are distributed in the way that the organic law applies, but without having a reflection in these public accounts. Here only around 20% of the funds will be collected which will modulate the allocation within the system, as foreseen by the organic law itself. I now turn to section 10, centralized recruitment, which has a budget of 317 million. It is somewhat less than what we had the previous year basically because it culminated in the process of acquiring low-emission vehicles zero-emission vehicles which, as you know, was an initiative taken by the Spanish Government, on the one hand, to help the car industry at a time of very significant decline in demand for this sector and, on the other hand, to make a renewal of the entire car fleet with vehicles that were exemplary in combating climate change, as we have also been moving to other areas of the Administration. The objective of this centralized procurement is to continue contracts already in place next year and to broaden the subjective scope. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, we are pursuing not only savings through the use of the economies of scale, but also the reduction of administrative burden on the various ministries - also on those autonomous communities that are in addition to centralized contracting - which allows us to apply cheaper costs for the purchase of the goods to which the Administration is obliged. Ladies and gentlemen, speaking of the administration's resources for the latest competence assigned to this ministry under the heading of the section, I would like to say to you that, regardless of the economic context in which we have been immersed in the war, the territorial entities have had the greatest support they have ever received from the resources from the system. Also in the year 2023 we will again support some of the commitments related to negative settlement, whose first delivery we made last year. As you know, local authorities have not only one year, but up to three years to deal with the return for the upward calculation that was made in 2020 so that territorial administrations could achieve the quality of the public services to which they were obliged. I repeat this every time I rise to this rostrum, because it seems to me to be an example of good practice and institutional loyalty: with a drop of more than 10 points of GDP we supply deliveries to the autonomous communities and the territorial entities with an increase - not a fall - of more than 1.5%. Subsequently, we have not claimed this money, but we have provided in the budget itself the amounts so that these administrations can return it without any kind of impairment or reduction in the resources derived from other services: health, education, street cleaning or'any other that have to affect these administrations. This is why the autonomous communities of common system will receive 135 273 million next year, 24% more than in the financial year 2022, and for the local entities these deliveries will translate into 23,235 million, 5% more than in the previous year, that is to say, 1113 million more. I conclude, ladies and gentlemen, as always, by appealing in this debate on budgets to the responsibility and to the height of the eyes of all the parliamentary groups. These accounts are objectively good. They are a magnificent instrument so that next year we can face not only the emergencies, but also the transformation challenges in which this country is immersed. They may or may not share some items, but they all aim to improve some of the content of the draft budgets. That is what we have been developing and that is what we have committed to with regard to the vote on the amendments next Thursday. But to that end, ladies and gentlemen, it is necessary that the draft budgets continue to be dealt with in the most reasonable way possible, because, as we always say, anything other than the fact that public accounts are recorded does not fit into this debate, even though some groups use it to mention it. The democratic health of this country also needs to be supported by public accounts so that citizens - not us - have better instruments. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I think it is good to try to stop undermining the rules of the democratic state, to continue to bet on improving the framework of coexistence and, therefore, to continue to make a useful policy to get away from this disaffection that some citizens feel by understanding that the debates in this House are alien to their everyday problems or by understanding that noise and noise are the most important thing in relations between political groups. Despite the noise that many are trying to make, I would like to acknowledge that in most of the political formations in this House we spend our time discussing, discussing and discussing between different people to enable public accounts to be made available for next year. This is what it is about: to regain the confidence of the citizens, to improve their lives and, ultimately, to make the importance of democratic representation prevail that we all display in order to obtain a utility for this country, and that utility means approving for the next year budgets with the amendments that are considered necessary, but budgets that give us more guarantees of success to avoid inequality, to promote the productive model and, ultimately, to make Spain bigger. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

Sánchez announced, with the emphasis and the roundness that characterizes him by affirming everything he does not intend to fulfil, a concrete commitment: to submit a proposal within eight months. Of course, it was unfulfilled. In December of last year, a partial proposal was sent to the Autonomous Communities to change the calculation of the adjusted population that serves to determine the distribution of the resources of the system and, after receiving the allegations of the Autonomous Communities, nothing has been heard again about the subject. I give you two very simple examples of that mismanagement. In conclusion, there are three failures that alone justify our amendment to return this section. Three failures to which the basic responsibility is attached in the preparation of budgets that not only do not serve the economic recovery of Spain, but also mortgage the well-being of all Spaniards for the coming years.
