ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u109	Thank you very much. We move on to the debate in section 16, Ministry of the Interior. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, for the Minister of the Interior.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u110	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before the plenary of the Congress of Deputies to present for the third time in this legislature section 16 of the general budgets of the State, corresponding to the Ministry of the Interior. In the same way as I did on previous occasions, I would like to thank my first words for the extraordinary work carried out by the more than 192 000 public employees who make up the department. They have been, as has been recalled on other occasions, three extremely complex years in which we have had to face all kinds of adverse situations. We practically started the legislature having to face a pandemic in which our public employees had the responsibility to ensure the restrictions of mobility in the different states of alarm, the provision and operation of essential services or the protection of the prison population and prison workers. Three years in which the National Civil Protection System had to deal with adverse weather events such as the damasto the drunk Filomena, in which we also had to turn the department's set of capacities to deal with the volcanic eruption of the island of La Palma or in which the Asylum and Refugee Office - which, by the way, this year celebrates its 30th anniversary - has had to manage nothing less than two humanitarian crises, Afghanistan and Ukraine. Three not easy years in which our more than 192,000 employees have reacted, as they always do, with surrender, work capacity and efficiency in resolving any crisis. Three years in which this House has played an extraordinary role, since it is no doubt you, ladies and gentlemen, who, by providing the Executive with budgetary powers, allow all these actions to be carried out with the appropriate resources and means. Therefore, in this third intervention, I would also like to thank the groups for their work in the successive general budgets of the State, since, by allowing them to be adopted, by proposing amendments and alternatives, they certainly demonstrate a firm commitment to a policy such as that of the Ministry of the Interior, which consists nothing more and nothing less than to give effect to the free exercise of our fundamental rights and our public freedoms, one of the greatest responsibilities of the State. Mr President, the general state budgets for 2023 are a road map for the social majority of our country, as has been recalled. These are budgets that strengthen and expand our welfare state, where 58.5%, i.e. almost 6 out of 10 euros, are allocated to social expenditure, as you know, and where the wage situation of public employees is improved, rising from 2.5 to 3.5% this year. In addition, a new model of growth of our economy based on intelligence, inclusion and, of course, sustainability is being promoted, with 72% of the European funds being devoted to the change of productive model in key strategic areas such as industrialization, digitisation or energy efficiency. They are therefore budgets that we can define as the social justice and prosperity accounts, but they are also the security budgets because, in line with the strategic vision that guides the elaboration of the general state budgets by 2023, from the Ministry of the Interior we have set up accounts aligned with national challenges - to collaborate in the ecological and digital transition and the drive for reforms and investments that improve infrastructure and technological capabilities within the framework of a society that tends to digitalize - and to consolidate the deployment of renewable energies while enhancing sustainable mobility. Similarly, these budgets seek to generate synergies that enhance the use of resources from the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism in order to achieve as fully as possible the recovery objectives that are being pursued. Therefore, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, we can say that the general budgets of the State for 2023 make it possible to achieve three key objectives. First, it is the budgets that consolidate the work of security recovery as a public good. Ladies and gentlemen, in these more than four years the current team of the Ministry of the Interior has had as a priority the reversal of the cut-off policies implemented in previous years. In this line, the general budgets for 2023 consolidate the path of recovery already begun with the budgets of 2021 and 2022. They also support the strategic lines of the 2030 Security Model, providing the agencies, departments and entities of the Ministry of the Interior with the necessary capacities and resources to improve resilience and responsiveness, and - more importantly - make security a strategic ally of the ecological and digital transition and thus of the change of productive model. To achieve this, we will achieve a record investment in staff, with more than EUR 8 billion, an increase of 6.5% over the previous year. It is an investment that will allow us to have, for example, 2169 new staff in the General Secretariat of Prison Institutions, which will be incorporated in 2023, from the public job offers of previous years. It will also increase the remuneration of government employees in the ministry by between 2.5 and 3.5%, which can be increased on the basis of different variables linked, as you know, to inflation or nominal GDP. It is also an investment that will allow us to incorporate in 2023 more than 4600 members of the State security forces and bodies, with a public employment offer consisting of almost 2500 new members of the basic National Police scale and 150 of its executive scale, as well as 2000 new cables and guards and 65 new officers of the Civil Guard. Therefore, we can say that with these general state budgets we will achieve in 2023 the maximum historical strength in the National Police and the Civil Guard, recovering more than 13,000 agents that were lost during the last governments of the People's Party. Thus, more agents in our peoples, more agents in our cities and more agents in our borders. And more agents also with more means, as reported by the Director General of the National Police and the Director General of the Civil Guard during their appearances last October 19th to expose the budgets. Also more agents, by the way, better paid, since with the approval by your ladies and gentlemen of the general budgets of the State by 2023 the retributive improvements of the forces and bodies of security of the State will reach an increase of 38 % -38 % - since 2018, an investment that will also achieve the protection of our rights and our freedoms. Here is, for example, the investment in the proper development of electoral processes, of 380 million euros. They also involve the consolidation of improvements in the care of victims of terrorism, implemented in the general budgets for 2022, the project of investment actions in cybersecurity and the completion of the Asylum and Refugee Office, which we started in 2018 and which will be reflected in the increase of the endowment linked to international protection with the inauguration of the new headquarters. In addition, there is investment in personnel and efficiency in the application that supports the management of the files or also the expandable credit of almost 17 million euros, aimed at strengthening the capacities of the National Civil Protection System in order to strengthen the protection of groups with greater risk of social and personal vulnerability in the event of disasters. Mr President, the second objective of the general state budgets in section 16 seeks to make security a strategic ally in the reforms of the productive model, in the ecological transition and in the digitisation of Spanish society, as indicated at the beginning. In this regard, the path initiated in 2021 will continue in key projects such as digital identity, mainly in the area of the so-called DNI 4.0, managed by the Directorate-General for Police, which will have a budget of 22.5 million euros. In addition, EUR 11 million will be allocated to training in digital skills for members of State security forces and bodies. These actions will also be reflected in the General Secretariat of Prison Institutions, which increases its Chapter 6 by 19,58 % in order to finance actions for the improvement of the energy performance of prison buildings, as well as for the digital transformation of the Prison Administration, as will their reflection in the Ministry's undersecretariat, where 4 million euros will be allocated between Chapters 2 and 6 for the consolidation of the digital plan and the launch of a quality office for digital transformation. Ladies and gentlemen, the third key objective of this section 16 is the consolidation of the autonomous bodies of the Ministry of the Interior. The General Directorate of Traffic increases staff costs by more than 5 million in order to meet the expansion of 114 members of the Civil Guard Trafficking Group. In addition, EUR 62 million is allocated to the long distance traffic management system project. These budgets also make steady progress in the consolidation of the National Police University Centre, which, as already done with the Civil Guard University Centre, will, together with the Cuenca Prison Studies Centre, be a determined commitment of the Government of Spain for training excellence, research and, which is also very important, for the deconcentration of agencies as a tool for territorial cohesion in the face of the demographic challenge. Ladies and gentlemen, 2023 will be the year of its consolidation and effective implementation. I am finishing, Mr President, and I want to do so by highlighting the infrastructure work of the Society of Prison Infrastructures and Equipment, SIEPSA. As you know, since January 2019, in addition to the infrastructure of the General Secretariat of Prison Institutions, CIEPSE has been entrusted with the implementation of the infrastructure plan and since 2022 it is the responsibility of this society to carry out nothing less than twenty-five actions in Civil Guard barracks, which correspond to the Energy Transition Plan of the General Administration of the State, financed from European funds. In 2023 SIEPSE will develop investments worth almost EUR 170 million, an increase of 16.77 % over 2022. This work materializes in the development of the so-called PASEO, which, as you all know, is the Plan of Amortization and Creation of Prisons, which will be endowed this year 2023 with 58200.000 euros for actions as relevant as, for example, the Centre Penitentiary North III or the comprehensive reform of the centre of El Dueso, among other activities, such as those scheduled in Jaén, Alhaurín de la Torre, Segovia, Villena, Badajoz, Aranjuez, Soto del Real, Alcalá de Guadaira, Daroca, Cuenca, Herrera de la Mancha, Brieva, Castellón, Alicante, Alhama or Pereiro de Aguiar, while in the field of infrastructures there are more than 97 million euros for the development of the Plan 2019-2026, in this year 2023, in twenty-four actions in the field of infrastructures corresponding to the forces and bodies of State security, which will be completed with twenty-five actions in different quarters of the Civil Guardia, as indicated in the framework of the General Administration. I conclude, Mr President, and do so by reiterating the alignment of section 16 with the main objectives of the general budgets of the State and recalling that these budgets, in addition to being a road map for the social majority of our country and being an instrument for welfare and prosperity, are also the encrypted and accounting expression of this Government with the recovery of security as a public good. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u129	Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u130	Thank you, President. The budgets reinforce the achievements made in the fight against the pandemic and meet the challenges of the present and the future in order to continue building a health of the future. I expressed this in these terms in my recent appearance before the Health Committee of this House. And, in order to achieve these objectives, he called for the support of this House. Ladies and gentlemen, these are budgets that our country needs, a country that is moving forward despite the difficulties of the times in which we live. We have a clear strategy for where we are going, with an unprecedented budgetary effort to strengthen our National Health System and, therefore, our welfare state. Ladies and gentlemen, one of the fundamental pillars of the welfare state is health. And talking about health means talking about health, and talking about health is, in the end, doing it about life. Hence the importance for this people's government, of improving the lives of each and every one of the people. This budget responds well to two questions. First, how we are dealing with current critical situations and how we are preparing our National Health System to deal with possible new situations. With these budgets we continue with the actions of this Government to reform, recover, advance and protect our citizens through the expansion and strengthening of the capacities of our health system. All: care, diagnosis, innovation, access to new medicines, professional development, prevention, health intelligence and resilience. Ladies and gentlemen, this is therefore a budget which is perfectly aligned with this objective which I have just mentioned. Turning already to the figures in section 26, I would like to highlight the commitment that these accounts consolidate the one already initiated by the Government of Spain - and also previous years - with an unprecedented investment, setting up a budget with a much higher budget than the average during the previous decade. This is because this Government has the vocation to strengthen the National Health System, consolidating actions that focus on equity in access and quality of care, regardless of where it resides. Ladies and gentlemen, the consolidated global budget for 2023, including the European funds, amounts to EUR 2746 million, an increase of 7.8% over 2022. The total budget of the department - that is, European funds plus autonomous bodies, ONT and AEMPS - without consolidation, that is, with internal transfers, amounts to 3102 million euros, assuming 6.5% more than in 2022. I am going to report on this budget forecast by management centres. With regard to care capacity and the Directorate-General for Public Health, I would like to highlight the Government's commitment to primary care. As you know, we have approved the Primary and Community Care Action Plan for the period 2022-2023, and this plan commits both the Government of Spain and the autonomous communities through their respective regional comprehensive plans to have a final budget for its effective implementation. Therefore, during the year 2023 the investment made in the year 2022, linked to this primary care plan, will increase somewhat more than twice. Thus, in the case of this Directorate-General for Public Health, in addition to maintaining the provision of 172 million euros of transfer to the Autonomous Communities and INGESA for primary, an additional 434 million is added, also for primary, in Chapter 7, for the improvement of infrastructures and equipment, such as health centres, clinics, conventional radiology rooms, dental or physiotherapy rooms, among others. This helps to improve the diagnostic and resolutive capacity of primary care, brings services closer, avoids displacement and provides a closer, faster and therefore more efficient response for citizens. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a very important investment, an investment of the present, but above all of the future, which will contribute to improving, together with the Autonomous Communities, primary care as a backbone of the National Health System. Secondly, I would like to emphasise the purchase of vaccines against COVID-19, with a budget of EUR 1316 million, in order to maintain protection for the most vulnerable people, with doses of vaccines adapted to variants and, on the other hand, to continue our firm reinforcement and our commitment to promote the plan of universal access to a solidary action of vaccines to other countries. I would also like to refer to another of the main contributions or new developments in these accounts, which is the EUR 50 million budget for the promotion of the Degree in Medicine. Since 2018, we have been making an important effort to increase specialized health training places, thus compensating for the prolonged decline in the supply of places between 2010 and 2017, which caused many of them to be lost, as in the case of the family medicine mir, which lost 1196 new specialists in this period from 2010 to 2017. That is why, since 2018, we have increased ESF places by 40% to the point that in the last calls the places offered exceed those who have graduated from medical schools. Hence our commitment to increase the places of the public faculties of Medicine with funding to adapt the places offered, as suggested by the Study of Offer-Necessity of Medical Specialists 2021-2035. I would also like to share with you, ladies and gentlemen, that these budgets continue to respond to the mental health needs of our people. As you know, in addition to updating the Mental Health Strategy, which had not been updated for twelve years, we approved, from co-government and unity, a mental health action plan for the period 2022-2024, and to continue it in the year 2023 we will increase its endowment, exceeding 43 million euros. This budget will allocate 3.3 million to consolidate line 024, attention to suicidal behaviour, which, as you know, has only served more than 68,400 calls in five months, of which 2660 were due to 112 and 1809 were active suicide interventions or at imminent risk of suicide. Therefore, you will understand, ladies and gentlemen, that more than figures are made, realities that show the relevance of this resource to all people. On the other hand, and in a complementary way, it is also necessary for autonomous health services to strengthen mental care at all levels, for which, in this budget for the year 2023, transfers to mental health amounting to 40 million euros are envisaged to our autonomous communities. Ladies and gentlemen, the Ministry of Health is also going to allocate EUR 12.4 million to this budget through European funds to strengthen preventive capacity and health promotion. Of these, 6.4 million will go to the FEMP for the creation or rehabilitation of healthy environments, so that the total amount for the period 2021-2023 will exceed 18 million euros for the FEMP for this objective, and the rest, another 6 million euros, will go as a transfer to autonomous communities for something as important as the prevention of cancer through population screening of colon and cervix. Thus, the amount of transfers to the autonomous communities in this concept will rise in the period 2021-2023 to 16 million euros. On the other hand, and also in relation to health prevention and promotion, activities as important as those related to rare diseases and to the prevention and control of AIDS and other STIs remain budgeted. Thus, in these budgets, we allocate EUR 2.8 million for the Strategy against Rare and Neurodegenerative Diseases, including ALS, which will be transferred directly to the Autonomous Communities. In addition, EUR 2 million is earmarked for AIDS and other STI-related grants. Alongside this, I think it is important to stress how through these budgets we are preparing our National Health System to deal with new critical situations in the future. We do this through the European funds, allocating an investment of 7.4 million euros, of which 3.2 million will go to the technological equipment of the State Agency for Public Health and 4.2 million to the surveillance network in public health. This investment is in addition to the more than 29 million we have allocated in the years 2021 and 2022. Also, within the health intelligence capacity, I would like to refer, within the General Secretariat of Digital Health, to the Government's commitment to digital health transformation as one of the tools that will make a decisive contribution to reducing the asymmetric development of health information systems throughout the country and will facilitate the enhancement of equity, cohesion and accessibility in the National Health System. Furthermore, as you know, ladies and gentlemen, the pandemic has highlighted the importance of data for decision-making and the protection of citizens' health, as evidenced by the success of vaccine development or the sequencing of successive variants of the virus. In addition, these data, through the national and later European health data area, have the capacity to offer new and better options for the prevention and health care of the National Health System as a whole through research and innovation. That is why we are going to allocate an investment of more than EUR 400 million in 2023 for this matter. The General Secretariat of Digital Health will count on almost 224.7 million euros, to which we must add 160 million euros for the digital transformation of primary care, financed in this case by the Secretariat of State of Public Service. This is a very important investment, ladies and gentlemen, which is intended to give continuity to the investment that we have already started in the general budgets for 2022, with EUR 70 million earmarked for this plan for the digital transformation of primary and Community care through the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, and on the other hand we also add, we also add 65 million more for the development of the health data area from the budget of SEDIA, which will be integrated into the future European Health Data Area, whose regulation is under analysis in the European Parliament and we hope that it can be closed during our next European Presidency in the six months of 2023. I also think it is relevant to comment, ladies and gentlemen, on the Digital Personalised Care Plan, with a budget of around EUR 100 million. As far as the Directorate General of Cartera is concerned, ladies and gentlemen, we continue to drive decisively the personalized medicine of precision. Thus, as a novelty, we include 100 million euros for the implementation of the very important genomic portfolio -. These are also transfers to the autonomous communities, ladies and gentlemen, which help to form the catalogue of benefits by guaranteeing access and care to all those who need it. This will allow, on the one hand, the implementation of new technologies and equipment for the extension of the use of biomarkers in public health facilities, which allow an adaptation of treatments by improving the selection and dosage of medicines in diseases such as cancer. The aim is to improve care while increasing equity in access throughout the national territory. On the other hand, it will also enable the development of the information system for genomic data analysis, which will revert to new knowledge to further advance. Ladies and gentlemen, another development that I want to share with you on the budget and which enhances equity is the improvement of healthcare for patients with rare diseases, including ALS. As you know, these complex diseases represent one of the most important challenges of care diagnoses in our health system. Thus, the budget envisaged the amount of €70 million to, among other actions, serve as a lever to the autonomous communities for the implementation of new neonatal screenings included in the portfolio of services of the National Health System. Also, the implementation of the latest updates of the orthoprosthetic supply catalog, such as the eye reader, or other communication systems for patients with severe neuromotor disorders or for the improvement of infrastructure and acquisition of equipment in the CSURs. We also allocate €37.4 million to cohesion funds to compensate precisely for the costs of the CSURs, which are, as you know, a paradigm of the cohesion of the health system, pooling resources and experiences and increasing patient safety and quality of care. Also, ladies and gentlemen, we continue with the oral health programme. We have added another EUR 44 million for the expansion of the common portfolio, as has been done in the budgets of this year 2022, which are included in the Primary Care Action Plan. And as far as INVEST is concerned, during the years 2021 and 2022, INVEST has concentrated the greatest resources with European funds, with almost 800 million euros, and many of the teams that make up this plan are already installed in the autonomous communities, are already in operation, of course at the service of the patients or the same, improving the diagnostic and therapeutic capacity of the National Health System and, therefore, the improvement of people's lives. I would like to point out that this budget of 2023 will have a budget of 200 000 euros to verify the correct implementation of all these equipment distributed in public hospitals throughout the Spanish geography, thus helping to narrow the gap between territories in access to high technology. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I refer in this section to the Collaborative Plan on the Implantation of Protontherapy, a public implantation for the most innovative treatments against cancer... ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u155	Thank you very much, Mrs Prieto. We continue with the debate in section 17, Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda. For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-22-CD221122.u156	Very good afternoon. Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, as you know, I appear before this House to present to you the draft budgets of the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda for the year 2023. I do so from the conviction that they are the most suitable to offer stability and confidence at times of enormous uncertainty; a stability that is incompatible with the policies of the save who might other governments practiced in their day, and a confidence that is achieved with expansive accounts that deepen the recovery and that offer a safety net to the middle and working class of this country so that no one will be released. Our goal is to alleviate suffering and not provoke it, persevering in the ecological and digital transformation of our economy. We are aware that we are not in possession of the absolute truth, so we have put our proposals to the attention of the various groups and accepted many of their amendments. Those who only listen to themselves will find it difficult to understand our willingness to dialogue and to agree. Negotiating budgets is a moral imperative. We will not give up any vote because what is at stake is the general interest, which is a greater good than we all owe. Unlike those who only seek to benefit the vast minority and support their privileges, this government is in no one's hands and only accountable to this Parliament. These are budgets that are consistent with our strategy. They are because the exceptional circumstances that we are experiencing as a result of the war that Russia has unleashed at the gates of Europe have not changed our road map. Moreover, we have been reaffirmed in the urgency of speeding up the reforms we have undertaken. In the face of climate change negators, including some right-wing and far-right Members of this House, we continue to bet on meeting the greatest challenge of our time, including transforming mobility, decarbonising our transport system and promoting public transport. These are measures, especially the latter, that are alleviating the economy of many families and that are helping to contain the price spiral. As it is still very high - I would like to point out in case some of the members of the PP have not yet noticed - Spain has the second lowest inflation in Europe today. I am therefore surprised at the announcement by the Community of Madrid that it will not extend the 50% reduction in transport charges by 2023 while benefiting higher incomes with tax reductions. As I said to you, these are budgets that are consistent with the mobility policies that we have been implementing and that give special prominence to railway investment. And they are also consistent with our commitment to public housing, whose social function we are going to enshrine by law so that the payment of a rent ceases to be the cause of impoverishment of many citizens. This justifies the fact that we have allocated the largest departure in history to housing. In the face of savage land liberalization, which is the only recipe that right-wing groups continue to preach, this Government maintains its intention to create a large public housing park for rent and to enhance residential rehabilitation, an activity that generates economic development and social cohesion. Finally, they are consistent in supporting the transformation of our cities, which is where the great battle for sustainability is waged and against economic and social inequalities. All of this leads me to ask you from the beginning to support these budgets, in which this ministry will have channeled the resources of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan that we manage completely. These General State Budgets for the coming year 2023, as I said, are the Government's road map for the social majority, whose main objective is the strengthening of the middle and working class through the defence of the welfare state, the modernization of the Spanish economy, support for young people and territorial cohesion. Already entering the section of the department's exhibition, the priority objectives that we have set ourselves in these budgets for the year 2023 from the ministry are the following. With regard to mobility, firstly, decarbonisation, promoting active mobility, as well as the use and efficiency of public transport by road, rail and zero-emission fleets; secondly, innovation and digitisation, deploying new technologies to digitise transport and, thirdly, accessibility, reducing public transport prices, improving reliability, security and infrastructure with a gender perspective, as well as enhancing territorial vertebrate. With regard to housing, the priority is to promote the construction of affordable or social rental housing to facilitate access to decent housing, especially for the most vulnerable, to whom we continue to protect. In the area of rehabilitation, we intend to improve the habitability and energy efficiency of households. And with regard to young people, facilitate their emancipation with rent subsidies and the increase in supply at affordable and limited prices. Strengthen aid and the protection of economically most vulnerable individuals and families in order to guarantee their right to decent and adequate housing. These are the lines on which we are already working, and it is enough to make a review of the activity of the last few days to see that, indeed, it is so. Regarding mobility, in addition to our commitment to bring the law of sustainable mobility to this House before the end of the year, we have presented the future station of Girona airport; we have committed to the tram of Palma; we have promoted the railway connection between the airport of Seville and the station of Santa Justa or we have put in service the new bus terminal in the T4, at the airport Adolfo Suárez Madrid-Barajas. We have also noted the progress made in the major works under way, such as the Mediterranean corridor or the variant of Pajares. In the area of urban agenda, we have begun the works of Madrid Nuevo Norte. In the area of sustainability, we have signed an important protocol for the development of green marine fuels. On the road we presented the energy strategy and granted the first package of aid for the rehabilitation of public buildings. And despite its importance, it is only a small demonstration of the actions that this Government is promoting through its commitment to society. In order to be able to continue this task, I then go on to detail the most significant data from the budget presented by this ministry. The consolidated budget of the MITMA group for 2023 in chapters 4, 6, 7 and 8 and investments of the entities amounts to EUR 21,273 million, an increase of 9 % over the current budgets. This means that we have presented the most sustainable and social budget, the most ambitious budget in the last 13 years, because they are 66% higher than those approved by the People's Party Government. The investment amounts to 16,550 million, the largest volume reported since 2010, doubling the previous Executive's forecast in 2018. In addition, I would like to highlight the fact that it includes €41 billion in transfers to the autonomous communities and local entities for sustainable mobility and for rehabilitation and social housing. With these budgets, which represent and account for 1.6% of GDP, we hope to generate 310,000 jobs. And they are also clearly sustainable because 60% of our investments have green labels. As regards the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, EUR 5070 million is included in this exercise. In the case of direct investment in the year 2023 it will have been committed in its entirety and with this budget we hope to increase the implementation to 2550 million euros, representing 33% of the whole plan. And in indirect investment we have already made transfers to the autonomous communities and to Ceuta and Melilla in the amount of 3315 million euros to make their investments or calls, which is 55% already. With this budget we also ensure the transfer of all funds to the autonomous communities and local entities. And, in addition, we have launched calls for local entities or companies worth EUR 2620 million that have already been awarded or are in process. With regard to housing and the urban agenda, we know that it is a priority of this Government and therefore, given the social urgency in the housing sector and the absence of public policies of the past, we dedicate the largest budget in history to housing: EUR 3467 million, 5% more than in 2022. To social housing, in particular, 1457 million euros are allocated, of which 836 correspond to the Plan of 100,000 housing for affordable rent and, among many other examples, we have the housing that is being developed in Barcelona, Ibiza, Valencia, Seville, Alicante, Mallorca - Son Busquets - or Madrid - Camp, as well as other activities in municipalities with less than 5000 inhabitants, such as Olvega in Soria or San Andrés del Rabanedo in León. In social housing we also allocate 421 million euros, which are allocated to the State Housing Plan in its different programmes, such as the rental of low-income people, victims of gender-based violence, those who have been released from their usual housing, homeless people or elderly or disabled people. And 200 million for the young rental bonus, which will benefit 70,000 young people between 18 and 35 years of age for the second year. On the other hand, we will allocate EUR 910 million to rehabilitation programmes. Residential rehabilitation and its fiscal incentives will have 880 million euros for large-scale regeneration of neighbourhoods and the urban environment; also to enable, as I said, fiscal incentives in habitual housing; as well as for the rehabilitation of houses and buildings; and also architectural rehabilitation, with 30 million euros, to act in numerous buildings and buildings representative of our cultural identity. Finally, EUR 11 billion is for loans to promote building rehabilitation policies and the construction of socially or affordable rental housing within the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. With all these budgets and actions we will contribute to the milestone of achieving and implementing 510,000 residential rehabilitation activities by the second half of 2026, which will reduce the demand for heating or cooling by 7.7% and reduce the consumption of non-renewable primary energy by at least 30%. We will also promote the construction and provision, as I told you, of 100,000 social rental or affordable housing; facilitate access to decent housing for more than 270 000 young people; and we will also contribute to enabling both autonomous communities and local authorities to rehabilitate public buildings, for which we have already transferred €480 million from the funds to the communities, which will finance 246 actions on an area of about 2 million square metres; and we will also transfer 600 million to local authorities, once the competitive competition call is resolved. I will also give you details of the main mobility items. In the case of the railways, it leads the investment of the department, with 7991 million euros, 18% more than last year's budgets. There is a 22% increase in that for the conventional and nearby network, up to EUR 3467 million; and also a 4% increase in high speed, with EUR 2694 million. Also, in turn, Renfe previews investments for an amount of 764 million euros; with them, we will put in service, in the first semester of 2023, the connection with Asturias and the second milestone of the high speed in Extremadura: the electrification of the stretch between Plasencia and Badajoz, which is also part of the Atlantic corridor. By the way, both corridors, the Atlantic and the Mediterranean, have in the budgets of the year 2023 a very similar planned investment: 1648 and 1695 million, respectively, according to our commitment, because both are indispensable for the economic development of our country. I do not want to forget the other performances in progress, of which I can quote, for example, the baipás de Almodóvar del Río, the outstanding works between Antequera and Granada, the high speed to Santander, the electrification of the Teruel-Zaragoza line, the Basque Y, the Murcia-Almería and a long etcetera. I would like to point out that a total of EUR 2200 million, 20% more than in 2022, is earmarked for the neighbourhood. This means tripling the investments of the previous Government. We do this because it is a public service in which more than 443 million annual trips are made, where we want to enhance the reliability and timeliness of the service, help reduce emissions and increase demand. Thus, the actions will be oriented towards the improvement and renovation of the track, the stations, electrification, the installations and the protection of steps at the level. On the other hand, we have allocated EUR 2666 million to roads, of which EUR 1548 million are for conservation, reversing the deficit of recent years and reaching the historical peak. In addition, pedestrian and cycling-oriented actions are planned, seeking the safety of the most vulnerable users, among other actions, with the humanization of journeys in the urban environment and the protection of wildlife to reduce abuse. A total of EUR 1206 million is allocated to the maritime mode in the budget. Ports play a key role in intermodality, logistics chain efficiency and decarbonization, so we will continue to improve terrestrial access and the intermodality of the port network, promote alternative technologies and energies, and boost investments in digitization and sustainability. Similarly, with regard to SASEMAR, we foresee the investment necessary to ensure the functioning of our maritime rescue system and the fight against pollution. The air mode also receives a total investment of 827 million euros. In the case of AENA, efforts will be made to ensure the recovery of traffic and efficient management of the airport network in terms of safety and quality. It will also continue to make progress in environmental sustainability as a backbone of all actions, especially with the photovoltaic plan, and, of course, innovation will be an essential part of service delivery. On the other hand, ENAIRE provides the basis for the recovery of air operations and their economic situation. Digitalisation, greater automation and the use of satellite technology will be key to driving the Single European Sky, with greater benefits in terms of sustainability and capacity as well. As far as sustainable mobility is concerned, the budget envisages funds to boost active and decarbonised mobility in our cities, amounting to EUR 860 million. With this, we are making progress, for example, in the creation of 550 kilometres of new bicycle lanes and in the conditioning of 450 kilometres of existing cycling lanes, as well as in the pedestrianization of streets, the establishment of low-emission zones in municipalities, the acquisition of zero-emission buses or the establishment of public bicycle rental systems. In order to achieve mobility accessible to all, more than EUR 3 billion is planned for transport aid; EUR 13 billion for rail public service obligations, near, rolling stock and half-distance; EUR 924 million for transport aid in extra-penisular territories; EUR 373 million for transport consortia; EUR 98.5 million for road passenger and freight allowances; EUR 24.5 million for large family allowances; and EUR 15 million for State concessions for regular road passenger transport. In addition, I would like to highlight that 660 million euros are allocated, as you know, to maintain the freeness of proximity, rolling stock and conventional half-distance, as well as to reduce by 50% the Avant fertilizers; and more than 15 million euros to finance new recurring Avant services, the future intermodal tickets, of which we are going to do a pilot experience between Soria and Calatayud; innovative measures of transport to demand for areas at population risk; and the new railway services of proximity between Palma del Río and Villa del Río, Illescas-Fonlabrada-Humanes, Málaga-El Chorro-Caminito del Rey, Murcia-Cartagena and Medina del Campo-Valladolid-Palencia. The continuity of free allowances is a good reflection of what we see as a service to citizens, in this case, as regards daily mobility. It is an unprecedented bet on public transport, with the great environmental benefits it entails, compared to the use of the private vehicle. We are talking about saving more than 350 million litres of petrol and avoiding the emission of more than one million tonnes of CO2 into the atmosphere throughout the year 2023. It is a bet that also has a positive impact on families' pockets. In fact, with the continuation of this measure in 2023, a family of four people can save between 1800 and 3000 euros over the next year. Although many said that this would be electoral and that the measure would not be worth anything, more than 2 million free allowances have already been issued, indicating the excellent reception by citizens. In the same vein, we must welcome one of the agreements reached in the context of the budget negotiations, which will also make state-owned bus lines free of charge, taking a step further in the area of mobility. Ladies and gentlemen, I conclude. We have put before this House a sustainable budget and for people, who approach people where they cannot get there, who manage to shorten distances, because never before has a government been as close to people as it is now. Behind the figures and budget lines there are people with names and surnames, who are the last recipients of all our actions, who occupy the heart of our policy, because we are going a step beyond the mere construction of the big infrastructures, we are focusing on the services we provide to the citizens and turning into the environment where they reside. In addition, we also help to cope with the climate emergency in which the planet is located, promoting the most sustainable modes of transport and seeking energy efficiency. Thus, we are getting the policies promoted by the ministry to contribute to the change in the economic and productive model that our country needs. We are advancing in social and territorial cohesion, helping those who need it most, the most vulnerable, and transforming our country, abandoning the radial conception and getting the transport system more and more malled; a transport system that, in addition, must give a revulsive turn towards intermodality, thinking about facilitating the mobility of people and goods in the best possible way, seeking optimization and also gaining in efficiency. These are the lines of work of our Government and the orientation that we have sought to put into this draft general state budgets for the coming financial year 2023. Finally, I would like to thank those political formations which, during the procedure of the draft budgets, have expressed their willingness to support them and which, moreover, are being or have already been implemented in the adoption of amendments. Actually, this is very good news. Expenditure is reduced simply by the positive evolution of the labour market, which allows us to maintain the level of unemployment protection and still reduce benefit expenditure by EUR 1192 million. In fact, ladies and gentlemen, excluding this reduction in unemployment expenditure, the MITES budget actually increases by EUR 523 million, because these are the largest funds we will have for the rest of the proceedings. Entering into detail the general budgets for the year 2023 of this ministry, the most relevant elements are those that follow. Firstly, we are going to significantly improve unemployment protection. I would like to thank all the groups who have understood the importance of what we have done in these general budgets, because yes, ladies and gentlemen, we have eliminated, we have repealed one of the biggest cuts that led to social protection by the People's Party. Remember those Fridays of pains in which they were subsequently cutting public unemployment benefit, and in one of the last royal decrees reduced the regulatory base from 180 days from 60% to 50%. Well, the progressive coalition government repeals this cut, and we can say that we return to the previous situation and, as of January, the Spanish stops and stops will be able to receive 60% of the regulatory base. This means that your benefit will be increased by EUR 100, in average terms, by around EUR 1000 per month. I think this is extraordinary news for our country. The increase is over 11%, as you see, which means that unemployed people in our country who receive a contributory benefit will maintain their purchasing power in the face of inflation that is truly impossible in our country. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, let me also point out that we have raised IPREM to EUR 600 per month, a measure that improves and expands the protection of the most vulnerable groups. I do not need to remind you, especially your ladies and gentlemen from the Committee on Labour and Social Security, that IPREM was frozen for more than ten years in the so-called austerity decade and now has an increase of about 12% since 2020. This is an increase which, in absolute terms, rises to more than EUR 66 per month. In the general case, the unemployment benefit will be increased from 1 January to EUR 480 per month, an increase of EUR 50 accumulated since 2020, also equivalent to an improvement of 11.7% in the amount of protection to those most in need. Always note that we are revaluing and increasing benefits by offsetting the impact above inflation, this was one of the objectives that the progressive coalition government had in designing these budgets. This is how we maintain the level of protection, but, as I said, we cut unemployment expenditure by 5.4% simply and thanks to collective work and the good development of the labour market, which, as I say, has more jobs and fewer unemployed people. Please note that the actual expenditure on unemployment benefits in 2020 amounted to EUR 34 984 million. In the 2022 budget, the expenditure on benefits that this House subscribed amounted to 22,093 million. And for this year, the forecast we put forward is 20,901 million euros. In short, we protect more people and improve protection by increasing the amount of benefits, but we reduce spending because the unemployment rate is set at 12.8% on average in 2022 and will fall to 12.2% on the annual average for 2023. It is certainly too high a rate, as I am always told, but, as I also always say, it is less than half of what we have suffered with the financial crisis in Spain since 2013. I repeat: less than half. Active employment policies will reach - and this is of great interest to the Autonomous Communities - their greatest figure in history, with more than EUR 8 billion, of which EUR 6847 million will be managed directly by the Public Employment Service, which will, as you know, be transformed into the employment law that is being debated in this House, with an increase of EUR 367 million. It is the third consecutive increase in such a way that, compared to 2020, the resources allocated to active employment policies, in short, to improving the employability of Spanish workers, have increased by 2231 million euros, an increase of 38,5% - I insist - compared to the first year of the legislature, 38,5%. Therefore, in 2023 we will maintain the amount of funds transferred to the Autonomous Communities so that they can deploy the new active employment policies. Remember that the Sectoral Conference on Employment and Labour Affairs in 2020 distributed 1944.5 million euros among the autonomous communities. In 2022, in addition to the Ministry's funds with the share of the training quota it manages, the figure has risen to 3751.7 million euros, an increase of 1807 million in these three years, which is the equivalent of 93%. In a legislature hit by successive crises, funds transferred to the autonomous communities for active policies have doubled to the highest figure in history. On the other hand, as you know, SEPE's budget is financed primarily by the unemployment and training quotas paid by workers; we are talking about the rights of workers. Well, in 2023 we made extraordinary progress in the self-financing of the budget, and I believe that this is a very important element in our country. In this way - this is also remarkable - the contribution of the State to balance the accounts of the Public Employment Service will no longer be necessary. We protect people, we have more jobs and more quality, but we are also more efficient in the Spanish government in economic management. In the fisheries chapter, in the budget of the ministry we dedicate a total of 110 million euros: 88.54 million, to which we must add 21.65 from the Plan of Recovery, Transformation and Resilience. To all this, we will devote the ministry's budget to this fishing party. There are, of course, very significant research projects. Another key section - I mentioned at the beginning - of the ministry concerns agricultural insurance policy. Agricultural insurance policy is undoubtedly one of the key elements for maintaining farm income and economic viability in a situation such as the current climate change situation and the increased occurrence of adverse and extreme natural events. We continue the growth path that we started in 2019, this year we will increase to 317.73 million euros the total amount of the grant of agricultural insurance, which is an increase of 23% for this year. This is the highest amount a Government has devoted to support for agricultural insurance. [[Applauds]] Indeed, this is a fundamental chapter because it gives stability and certainty at a time of particular difficulties and, in addition, it considerably reduces the amount of premiums, the cost of premiums for farmers. In addition, we have managed to prevent the possible rise, for reasons of imbalance between compensation and income, in relation to certain budget lines, from being unbalanced. Ladies and gentlemen, we believe that this is an appropriate budget for the present time within our agri-food sector - agriculture, livestock and fisheries - as well as for the agri-food industry, firstly, to consolidate its sustainability and profitability; secondly, to support its resistance to weather conditions and in an international context as volatile as the one we are currently living in; thirdly, to transform the productive structure of the sector and to respond to new needs and challenges; and finally, to ensure Spain's social and territorial cohesion, which is how we consider and how we achieve that food security that this House and that the Government of Spain undoubtedly defends, which means that we are able to provide our citizens every day with food and drink in sufficient quantity and quality and, of course, at reasonable prices. With the aim of centralizing efforts around the Plan to boost the sustainability and competitiveness of agriculture, livestock and fisheries, we propose to create this advisory office because we believe that it would be very important that the Government's work in the General Courts contributed and ensure that the regulation we make here, and the regulations and actual decrees approved by the Government, are always aligned with the latest available scientific evidence. The agricultural sector in Spain faces several major risks, first of all generational relief, because young people do not really see this sector as attractive. We want to help them and believe that in this way we could attract many of them, it can be a way to attract new young people who want to dedicate themselves on this path to the agricultural and livestock sector. Secondly, by adapting the sector to the effects of climate change and to a context in which serious disasters such as droughts, floods or storms. New trends must be adopted by farmers and livestock farmers, and with this office there would be an opportunity to use better information, and that all the laws that we could manage and process here would always have that content and scientific backing of that office. As I said, this amendment has been vetoed by the Government, but we did have to put it into value because we believe that the future of the agricultural sector can and must be sustained within the scientific sphere. We also proposed - and it has been vetoed - a new additional provision to create an R+D+i program in southeastern Spain. The State Agency of the Higher Council for Scientific Research was entrusted with the implementation and implementation of an R & D & I programme in the south-east of Spain, with a special impact in the province of Almeria, and with reference to intensive farming with three lines of action: collection, treatment and recycling of waste products and by-products from this agricultural activity, with particular reference to plastic and organic waste; reduction of the environmental impact and recovery of degraded spaces by activity; and an urgent and efficient increase in the production of desalinated water for irrigation and human consumption. We thus support the intensive farming activity, which is a fundamental economic pillar in the southeastern Spain, revealing itself crucial to the Spanish balance of payments for the incessant and growing activity. However, the constant growth of this area dedicated to this activity and the constant increase in production generates not a few frictions with the supreme value of environmental protection. That is why we thought it was interesting that this R & D & I plan, at least, could be voted on here for these budgets. We also tabled an amendment as an additional new provision for the creation of an active promotion plan for the GECCO programme, collective management of recruitment at source. It seemed to us that an active promotion plan for this programme with the Spanish-American countries with which Spain has not yet signed cooperation instruments in this area such as Bolivia, Chile or Costa Rica could be very interesting. Collective management allows the hiring of workers who are not located or resident in Spain, selected in their countries of origin from the generic offer submitted by employers, in order to be able to carry out the work in Spain and to do so with the legal support that this plan would give both farmers and those hired in the countries of origin. Finally, I would like to point out that we have tabled an amendment, a new additional provision to strengthen health and plant health controls. We propose a plan to strengthen health and plant health controls because a commercial opening that is not accompanied by real control of plant health and health controls puts at stake the competitiveness and balance that must exist between imports, exports and our internal market. We wanted to make it clear that many of the amendments should not have been vetoed because they were very interesting, but we will continue to work so that in the future we can implement some of these plans. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]
